The walker's collision stands on the same tiles ([scene_collision](scene_collision.md)): its ground is the tiles' source at the drawn pair's times, and its `max_ground_error_m` is measured against the finest tile level. A build without the world capability follows the camera by the ring's first-fill rule. `content/test-scenes/desert-endless/` is the scene to fly. The summary's `time_lapse` block has one level per far level and per ring after the scene's (undrawn) grid and the `rings` block above with `layout` `tiles` and `far_levels`, the far levels drawn past the outermost ring (`--terrain-far`, [renderer](renderer.md#ground-to-the-horizon); 0 under `--terrain-rings`); a rebuild is one tile set's change, its chunks the tiles. Over the protocol the count is `RenderSettings.terrain_far_levels` (version 11; null is the tunable's, as here without the flag) and `RenderGroundStats.far_levels` reports it.

**In an `--interactive` window `,` and `.` step the world's rate the sand rides** (since 2026-10-20; the rate the motion runs at is `--time-rate` times the world's, which `,` and `.` step down and up its ladder 0, 1/8, 1/4, 1/2, 1, 2, 4, 8, 60, 600, 3,600, 86,400 while the sand moves — [the two rates](#the-two-rates-on-four-keys)), through `TerrainMotion::set_rate` and so through every rule above: game time runs on at the new rate, the sand slows down within a second or speeds up over one to three without outrunning the fields already on their way, comes to rest at zero rather than stopping dead, never steps and never moves a vertex past the per-frame bound, and the next field is timed at the new rate ([renderer](renderer.md#changing-the-rate-while-it-runs) has the rules and what the tests hold). So the motion exists to be set going, an interactive window draws a dune terrain as terrain levels even at `--time-rate 0` (`RenderSettings::time_rate_live`) — the pool's 768 MiB floor, the rest pose evaluated once at load and no cone test on the terrain, whether or not the keys are pressed, and the same picture. Offscreen runs ignore the keys, so their pictures stay a function of their frames.

The summary line (the window's and the offscreen one's) gains a `time_lapse` block, `null` without the flag: `rate` (the one it ended at), `start_rate` and `rate_changes` (the keys'), `fraction`, `min_step_s`, `max_step_s`, `lead`, `wait`, `game_time_s`, the surface clock's `latency_s` (L, game seconds; 0 offscreen, where the sand keeps game time and waits for its fields), `max_latency_s`, `latency_frames` (L in frames at the rate), `lag_s` (how far behind game time the sand stood at the end), `stopped_frames` (frames it stood still after it first moved) and `braked_frames` (frames the clock slowed it for a late field), the fields' `upload_bytes` and `upload_ms` (GPU), and `levels`, one object per terrain level with its `spacing_m`, `surface_s` (the game time the sand stands for), `time_a`, `time_b` and `blend` (its pair of fields and where between them it is), `padding_m` (how far its sand is from the rest pose its bounds were fit to), `evaluated` and `installed` (fields), `held`, `capped` and `waited` (frames the sand stood still for a late field, was slowed by the per-frame bound, and waited for one offscreen), `late` (fields timed further out than the displacement rule to keep up), `max_move_m` (the most any vertex moved in a frame), `max_delta_m`, `max_step_s`, `max_lag_s`, `last_eval_ms`, `mean_eval_ms`, `last_turnaround_s` and `max_turnaround_s` (real seconds from the level wanting a field to its being ready to draw: the evaluation and the wait behind the other levels'), and `last_hash` (16 hex digits over the last field's bytes: two offscreen runs of the same `--frames` compare by it).

**With `--benchmark`, every frame's record carries a `terrain` object** (`engine.scene.FrameTerrain`, `FrameRecord` version 7; `null` without a time-lapse): `game_s`, `latency_s`, `lag_s`, `rate` (the rate in force that frame, since `FrameTerrain` version 2: the keys change it), `speed` (the sand's advance that frame over the true one: 1 is the rate) and `levels`, one `TerrainLevelFrame` per level with `surface_s`, `time_a`, `time_b`, `blend`, `move_m` (the most any of its vertices moved that frame), `held` (it stood at its b with no next field), `late` (the clock braked for its newest field) and `ahead` (fields after b it had). It is what a stop, a sprint or a slow-down is found by, a frame at a time — in a window with `--interactive --benchmark`, where the clock runs, and offscreen, where it does not.

| Tunable | Default | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| `renderer.terrain.move_fraction` | 0.25 | the share of a level's spacing the fastest band may travel between two fields, and any vertex may move in a frame |
| `renderer.terrain.min_step_s` | 1 | game seconds between two fields at least (a minute until 2026-09-28, which let a storm with a transport gain cross-fade at the game's own rate; [renderer](renderer.md#the-dunes-in-time-lapse)) |
| `renderer.terrain.max_step_s` | 2,592,000 | and at most (30 days) |
| `renderer.terrain.lead` | 1.5 | in a window, how far ahead of a field's turnaround (its evaluation and its wait behind the other levels') the next field is timed; twice that for the scene's grid, which has room for one field after b to a ring's two |
| `renderer.terrain.pool_mib` | 768 | the pool's budget at least, for a scene whose terrain moves: an entry the pool cannot place draws its rest pose, which for a terrain is a jump |
| `renderer.terrain.upload_mib` | 8 | in a window, the megabytes of evaluated fields copied onto the device a frame; a field is taken only once all of it is there (offscreen, everything the next frame) |
| `renderer.terrain.ring_slack` | 1.15 | with `--terrain-rings`, the room a ring's slots and arenas keep above the largest ring the layout rule can make, from the chunks built at load |
| `renderer.terrain.tile_slack` | 1.5 | with the world's tiles, the room each tile's slot and its level's arenas keep above the largest tile the first layout built ([renderer](renderer.md#the-ground-from-the-worlds-tiles)) |
| `renderer.sun.azimuth_deg` | 48.37 | where the stand-in sun stands without `--sun`: degrees in the ground plane from +x towards +z |
| `renderer.sun.elevation_deg` | 53.03 | and degrees above the horizon (the two defaults are the sun every frame has always had) |
| `renderer.sun.rate` | 0 | game seconds per real second the sun's day runs at without `--sun-rate`; 0 is a still sun, exactly the one above |
| `renderer.sun.arc_tilt_deg` | 40 | the tilt of the day's arc: the celestial pole's elevation over the northern (−z) horizon, which is the latitude (0 the equator, 90 a pole) |
| `[` / `]` ladder (not a tunable) | 0, 60, 600, 3,600, 8,640, 86,400 | the sun's rates an `--interactive` window's `[` and `]` step through, game seconds per real second |
| `,` / `.` ladder (not a tunable) | 0, 0.125, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8, 60, 600, 3,600, 86,400 | the world's rates `,` and `.` step through, which the dunes ride at `--time-rate` times; from a rate between two rungs a key goes to the rung on its side |
| `renderer.terrain.ring_upload_mib` | 16 | with `--terrain-rings`, in a window, the megabytes of a re-centre's chunks copied onto the device a frame (one chunk at least): under 2 ms of copy at the RTX 5090's 9.4 GB/s, and the erg's 91 MB re-centres in about six frames |

`renderer.terrain.time_step_s` and `renderer.terrain.time_min_frames`, the step rule of the first time-lapse (which evaluated at every game day and uploaded nothing), are gone with it. Over the protocol it is `render.load`'s `settings.time_rate` (`RenderSettings` version 8, and the lights' `orbit_lights`, `sun_azimuth_deg` and `sun_elevation_deg` from version 9): the host builds the terrain level with it, but since 2026-10-03 its `render.*` methods run the motion too — through the renderer's `MovingGround`, the object engine-view drives — waiting for its fields as an offscreen engine-view does, and `terrain_rings` and `terrain_tiles` are settings there (version 10; a host has no world ring, so the tiles follow the camera by the ring's first-fill rule); `clock` runs the sun's day, and `render.benchmark`'s `flythrough` carries the `time_lapse` and `sun` blocks engine-view's does ([renderer](renderer.md#one-request-two-hosts)).

## engine-view --world: a streamed scene

A scene whose ruins are too many to read whole — a thousand ashlar buildings are 33 million (instance, cluster) pairs, twice what a visibility id can name — is drawn **a tile at a time** round the camera by the world capability ([world](world.md)): its `ruins` entries are not assembled when the scene is read, but tile by tile as the camera's tile ring takes tiles in, in the representation the ring asks for — laid block by block in the inner ring, the kit's sections with their debris in the middle one, the walls alone beyond — and dropped when the ring lets them go. The renderer takes the tiles' instances between frames as a **tail** behind the scene's own ([renderer](renderer.md#instances-that-come-and-go)), so the terrain and the meshes stay resident and nothing is read again.

```powershell
engine-view --scene scene.json --world --camera-path path.json --offscreen `
            --benchmark run.jsonl --world-log world.jsonl [--world-handover handover.jsonl]
```

- **`--world`** streams a scene file with the capability's rings (1.5 tiles in blocks, 8 in sections, 24 in walls only; 32 m tiles; hysteresis 0.15; 8 activations and 16 deactivations an update). **A scene file's `"world"` block** (`engine.scene.WorldRings`: `tile_size`, `rings` of `{radius, ruins}` innermost first, `hysteresis`, `simulated`) does the same with its own rings, and `"world": {}` is the defaults; without either the scene is read whole, as before. A ruins entry's own `tile_size` must be the world's, or the file is refused ("must be one grid"): a ring tile would otherwise hold parts of four of the entry's.
- **The camera is the observer.** In a flight the ring updates before every frame from that frame's own camera (`FlightOptions::before_frame`), so the frame draws what the ring holds for it; each repeat and each warm-up starts from an empty ring filled without a budget, so every repeat flies the same world. Marker captures and the final `--capture` fill the ring completely first. **In the window** it follows the camera a frame behind — the update runs before a frame is begun, from the previous frame's camera — which is the one frame of latency a game's world has too.
- **`--world-log <out.jsonl>`** writes one `engine.world.TileFrame` per update — the repeat, the path frame, whether the frame was recorded, the tick; the tiles scored and active and the active tiles per ring; the events by kind, the refusals and the changes the budget held over; the ring's microseconds and each consumer's calls, milliseconds, worst call and commit; and the scene's instances and pairs, the buildings and the ruins' instances after it — then one `engine.world.TileWorldSummary` (`format` `engine.world-log.v1`): the rings, the totals, the ring's median, p99 and worst microseconds, and the placements consumer's buildings — the ruins' assembled and laid, by the representation their generator drew — dropped and refused with the milliseconds of each kind of work and of the renderer taking the tail (`insert_ms`, `insert_ms_max`, `handed`). The same summary is the `world` field of the flight's `FlythroughSummary` (version 5), and every `FrameRecord` carries the scene's `instances` and `pairs` at that frame (version 5), since a streamed scene's change from frame to frame — and, since version 6, `table_slots`, `table_pairs` and `hole_pairs`, what the frame wrote into the renderer's table set and how much of its cull dispatch no instance holds, with the writes' GPU time in `gpu_ms.tables` ([renderer](renderer.md#instances-that-come-and-go)).
- **`--world-handover <out.jsonl>`** (offscreen, with a camera path) flies the path once more after the timed flight, one frame at a time and untimed, and for every tile an update moves to a ring that draws it differently draws that frame **twice** — as the update left it, and with only that tile's instances put back as they were, everything else the update did being in both — each drawn once before it is captured so its visible pairs are its own, reading back colour, ids and depth. It writes one `engine.world.TileHandover` per tile: the tile, the two rings and representations, its distance; the pixels the tile's own instances drew before and after (found through the ids); the pixels covered in one picture and not the other (`coverage_changed`), covered in both at depths more than a thousandth apart (`depth_changed`) and, of those, more than a hundredth (`depth_moved`: geometry that moved, not relief), and whose colour changed by more than 8 of 255 (`color_changed`); `depth_changed` again as a **displacement histogram** (`depth_bins`: moved by 0.1–0.3%, 0.3–1%, 1–3%, 3–10% and more than 10% of the distance, which at 47 m is 5 cm, 14 cm, 0.47 m, 1.4 m and 4.7 m); of the changed pixels, those where either picture shows the tile's **rubble** — debris members or fallen blocks, from `PlacementTiles::tail_tags`, the tag the ruins generator gives rubble — rather than its walls (`rubble_changed`); the frame's visible pairs before and after; and the tile's instances and pairs before and after. It is **the pop a handover costs without a cross-fade**, which is the number [E35](../experiments/e35-world-streaming.md) needed, and the histogram and the rubble share say what the pop is made of.
- **Refused**: `--world` with `--deform`, `--animate`, `--morph-animate`, `--reference` or `--verify-occlusion` (a tail is rigid, and the occlusion check draws a second upload of the scene that no ring fills); `--raster rt` and `--shadows rt` are not refused since 2026-10-12, when the renderer's tail took the ray tracing chain and the pool ([renderer](renderer.md#instances-that-come-and-go)), `--world-handover` without `--offscreen` and a path, and all three flags in a build without the capability (exit 2). A scene file's `world` block is only known once the file is read, so it meets the last four with a refusal then (exit 1), and `--deform` the way the renderer resolves any dynamic scene: the deformation of the tail off, with a warning.

`--census` and `--census-pixels` count a tail's meshes through the GPU scene's own table, so a census of a streamed scene names the ruins' meshes like any other. `apps/engine_view/tests/ruins_view_tests.cpp`'s case "a streamed world draws the ruins its ring holds, as a whole read does" draws one ruins entry streamed and read whole and holds the two pictures to each other.
## `--interactive`: a camera somebody flies

`engine-view --interactive` (with `--scene` or `--mesh`, `--views`, `--shadows` and every other rendering flag) puts a fly camera in the window, driven by the keyboard, the mouse and a gamepad, and makes the whole session recordable and replayable bit for bit. It is the first thing in the engine that a person steers, and it is built the way a game will be: window events become `input::RawEvent`s once, at the edge; bindings are an `input::ActionMap` that is data; and the camera integrates at a fixed tick from an `input::InputState`, never from the window and never from a frame's duration ([input](input.md), plan 05 §5.10). The camera is `apps/engine_view/fly_camera.h`, the edge `window_input.h`, the title's numbers `pacing.h`.

```powershell
engine-view --scene content/test-scenes/desert-overlook/scene.json `
            --camera-path content/test-scenes/desert-overlook/camera-path.json `
            --interactive --record-input session.jsonl --benchmark session-frames.jsonl
engine-view --replay-input session.jsonl                                   # the same flight, in the window
engine-view --replay-input session.jsonl --marker-captures markers/        # offscreen, a PNG per marker
engine-view --replay-input session.jsonl --benchmark replay.jsonl          # offscreen, the path as a benchmark
```

**The default bindings** (`content/input-maps/engine-view.json`, which is `view::default_fly_map()` byte for byte — a test holds them together; `--input-map <file>` flies with another):

| Action | Kind | Keyboard and mouse | Gamepad |
|---|---|---|---|
| `move` | Axis2 (x right, y forward) | W / S forward and back, A / D left and right | left stick (deadzone 0.15) |
| `lift` | Axis (world up) | E or Space up, Q or Left Ctrl down | right trigger up, left trigger down |
| `look` | Axis2, read as a **distance** | pointer motion while the window holds the pointer | — |
| `turn` | Axis2, read as a **rate** | — | right stick (radial deadzone 0.15) |
| `fast` | Button (×`view.fly.fast`, 4) | Shift | left stick click |
| `slow` | Button (×`view.fly.slow`, 0.25) | Alt | right stick click |
| `capture` | Button | Escape: give the pointer back; with it already given back, end the session (below) | Back |
| `marker` | Button | M: remember this tick's camera | North (Y on an Xbox pad) |
| `sun_slower`, `sun_faster` | Button | `[` and `]`: the day's length a rung down or up its ladder (still, 24 h, 4 h, 1 h, 24 min, 4 min, 1 min, 10 s: sun rates 0, 1, 6, 24, 60, 360, 1,440, 8,640 game s a real s; the names are the map's, kept since R88) | — |
| `dunes_slower`, `dunes_faster` | Button | `,` and `.`: the world's rate a rung down or up its ladder (0 to 86,400 world s a real s, [the two rates](#the-two-rates-on-four-keys)), which the dunes ride; the names are the map's, kept since R88 | — |
| `walk` | Button | F: walk on the ground under the camera, or fly from where it stands ([Walking](#walking)) | — |
| `jump` | Button | Space, while walking (flying, Space is `lift`) | South (A on an Xbox pad) |
| `exposure_darker`, `exposure_brighter` | Button | `-` and `=`: a sky's exposure half a stop darker or brighter on top of its rule (or of the held value) | — |
| `exposure_hold` | Button | `0`: hold the exposure at the value the last frame was drawn at, or give it back to the rule | — |

`look` and `turn` are two actions because a mouse says how far and a stick how fast: `look` is read through `InputState::delta2`, the tick's pointer pixels unclamped, and `turn` through `axis2`, a position times `view.fly.turn_rate` per second — one action could not be both without capping how far a mouse can turn in a tick at one pixel's worth.

**The pointer, and the two Escapes** (2026-09-27, the owner's ask after his fly-throughs). **A click in the window, or the window gaining focus, takes the pointer** — relative mode, the cursor hidden and held (`window::Window::set_relative_mouse`) — because switching to the window is switching to fly it; a window opened in front of everything therefore starts with it. **Escape once gives it back**: the cursor shows, the camera stops turning with the mouse, and the keys and a pad still fly it. **Escape again, with the pointer already given back, ends the session** — the frame it was pressed in is drawn and presented and the run ends as a closed window's does, the summary printed and `--record-input`'s log and `--benchmark`'s file written. Losing focus gives the pointer back too, and coming back takes it again. Escape is the `capture` action, read at the tick it was pressed on like any other, so the two presses are ordered by the ticks and a recording holds both; the window's close button still ends the session at any time. **An injected run** (`--inject-input`, below) never takes the real pointer and ignores the desktop's focus: it starts as a window with the focus would — holding the pointer — and then follows its log's clicks and Escapes alone, so what its Escape does is a function of the log. (Until 2026-09-27 the window started without the pointer, Escape took it and gave it back, and nothing but the close button ended a session.) **Motion before relative mode is on is not looking** (2026-10-03): the session takes the pointer when it sees the click or the focus, and the window turns relative mode on at the end of that frame, so the motion polled in between — and any queued before the mode came on and polled after — is the absolute pointer's, whose delta runs from wherever the cursor last was: the owner's `--walk` once started facing somewhere else after one such event. The edge therefore looks only at motion the platform stamped after the mode came on (`view::PointerGate`, window_input.h; `window::Event::timestamp_ns` against `Window::relative_mouse_since_ns`), drops the rest before it is fed or recorded, and lets the capture alone decide in an injected run, which takes no real pointer, and on a platform that refuses the mode. `fly_tests.cpp` holds it: a 900-pixel jump before the mode and a queued 120 after it turn the session by nothing, three pixels after it by three pixels.

### The two rates on four keys

**`[` and `]` step the day's length; `,` and `.` step the world's rate** (2026-10-20, the owner; roadmap R88; [renderer](renderer.md#one-world-clock-and-the-days-length)), a rung of a ladder each press from whatever the flags started them at; from a rate between two rungs a key goes to the rung on its side, and past an end it stays there.

| keys | rate | ladder | flags |
|---|---|---|---|
| `[` `]` | **the day's length**, in real seconds — the sky's rate, `sun_rate = 86,400 / day_length_s` | still, 24 h, 4 h, **1 h**, 24 min, 4 min, 1 min, 10 s (sun rates 0, 1, 6, 24, 60, 360, 1,440, 8,640) | `--day-length <s>`, `--sun-rate <r>` |
| `,` `.` | **the world's rate**, world seconds a real second: the water, the smoke and the fire's flicker, the sway, the cloud drift, and the dunes' time-lapse with them | 0 (a pause), 1/8, 1/4, 1/2, **1**, 2, 4, 8, 60, 600, 3,600, 86,400 | `--world-rate <r>`; the dunes run `--time-rate` game seconds a world second |

**Why these two.** The owner's framing: inspect the world's events — the sand moving, a fire's smoke, the waves — at any speed without the sky strobing, and watch the sky at any day length over a world at real speed. The day is measured in real seconds, so `,` and `.` never move the sun: a pause holds every animation while the sun keeps its pace, and a fast-forward of the dunes no longer turns the screen into a day-night strobe. The day's ladder has a sixty-minute day as a rung because that is the production target, two presses from a real day; it stops at a 10 s day, the fastest the sky is worth watching at. The world's ladder has slow motion for looking at a flame or a frond, real time where a tester steps back to, and the minute, ten minutes, hour and day a second the dunes were watched at (at `--time-rate 1` these are the rates the dunes' own keys used to give them; `--time-rate` has no key of its own any more, and stays a flag and a state-file value). **The actions keep their names** — `sun_slower`/`sun_faster` and `dunes_slower`/`dunes_faster`, map revision 5 unchanged — because the committed default map's hash and the committed sessions are pinned on them; the dunes' pair now steps the world's rate. Both clocks run on the session's ticks at their rates, so a change changes how fast a clock runs and never where it stands; the dunes follow a world rate through `TerrainMotion::set_rate` at `time_rate × world_rate` ([renderer](renderer.md#changing-the-rate-while-it-runs)). Each change prints one line on stderr (`engine-view: day 1 h (sun x24 game s a real s; was 4 h)`, `engine-view: world x8 world s a real s (was x4); the dunes with it`), the title says the sky's hour, the day and the world's rate (below), and the summary's `sun` block ends with `rate`, `start_rate`, `rate_changes`, `day_length_s`, `world_rate`, `world_start_rate`, `world_rate_changes` and `world_time_s`; the `time_lapse` block's `rate` is the dunes' per real second, the product. They are actions of the map like the camera's and are recorded with it; **a replay in the window presses them where its recording did**, and an offscreen replay of a session without `--game` runs at its start rates (its pictures stay a function of its frame index); a `--game` session's clocks are the runner's and are stepped by the keys at their ticks in every replay (below). Keys only: a player's map can bind a pad's d-pad.

**A recording carries its clocks** (the header's `clock` block, every live session since 2026-10-20): `sun_rate`, `day_length_s`, `world_rate`, `time_rate` and the `--time-of-day` hour when one was given. A replay runs on them unless its command line names its own (`--sun-rate`/`--day-length`, `--world-rate`, `--time-rate`, `--time-of-day`), so it starts the day, the world and the dunes where the live session did and the keys step them from there; a header without the block replays at the flags' rates, as every recording before it did.

**The map's revisions.** The four keys were appended to engine-view's map as its second revision (`view::k_fly_map_revision`), which moved its hash — and a log names the hash of the map it was recorded against. So a replay given no `--input-map` flies with **the revision its log names** (`view::default_fly_map_for`): every session recorded before 2026-09-27, and the committed synthetic fixture, replays with the first revision, the eight camera actions, exactly as it did, and its keys press nothing new; a log recorded against a player's own map still needs that map. The walk mode's `walk` and `jump` are the **third revision** (2026-09-28, [Walking](#walking)), under the same rule: a session recorded against the first or second replays with it, and its F presses nothing. The exposure keys `-`, `=` and `0` are the **fourth revision** (2026-09-30, [renderer](renderer.md#exposure)), and the game actions `interact` (E, a pad's West), `use` (Q) and `game_1`..`game_4` (1 to 4) the **fifth** (2026-10-16, [--game](#--game-running-a-game-module)), which the camera never reads; each press prints its line on stderr (`engine-view: exposure +0.5 stops on the sky's rule`), and without a sky they say so and do nothing. The sign conventions are the camera paths': yaw about +y counter-clockwise from above, pitch about the camera's right axis, up positive, forward -z at zero — so the orientation is `quat_from_euler(yaw, pitch, 0)`, the same `CameraKey.rotation` a path key holds, and a recorded session's camera at any tick can be written as a path key without a sign flipping. `move` flies along the look direction, pitch included; `lift` is world up.

**Where a session starts**, in order: **`--start <x,y,z> <yaw,pitch>`** — metres, and degrees in the conventions above (`--start 0,4,900 0,-5` is the desert overlook's approach, 4 m up, looking north and a little down), written into the header as exactly those angles in radians, and the position **as the doubles typed** (since 2026-10-05: `--start -419055.125,90,-66781.671875 ...` starts there, not on the float32 3.1 cm grid beside it), anywhere a world cell can name it — a coordinate past ±1.37e11 m (`k_world_extent_m`, `world_cell_valid`) is refused with a sentence and exit 2, where until then anything past 1e7 m was; else **`--camera-path`'s first frame**; else **the first frame of the scene file's own camera path** (`Scene.camera_path`, schema version 2, a path relative to the scene file — the desert overlook names `camera-path.json`, so it starts 900 m south of the oasis looking north at the saddle, 2.5 m over the dunes); else the orbit camera with its near plane brought in to at most 5 cm, since a camera that flies in needs one. The orbit frames the whole scene's bounds, which is right for a mesh and was 9.4 km out for the 5 km desert: the first owner session began there, over a minute of flying from anything even with Shift held ([the write-up](../experiments/first-interactive-session-2026-09-24.md)), which is why a scene can now say where it is meant to be seen from. The scene's path is read for its first frame only and never flown. `--start` is refused with `--camera-path` (both say where to start) and with `--replay-input` (a replay starts where its recording started).

**The owner's landmarks in a session.** `--overlay <manifest>` works with `--interactive` exactly as with a flythrough ([Flythroughs](#flythroughs)): the scene's placeholders that name an overlay hash are replaced by the manifest's files, hashed and refused if their bytes disagree. A session recorded without it flew the committed Khronos stand-ins, which is what the first one did:

```powershell
engine-view --scene content/test-scenes/desert-overlook/scene.json --interactive `
            --overlay D:/workspace/game_engine_local/scenes/desert-overlook/landmarks.json `
            --width 11520 --height 2160 --views surround3 --record-input session.jsonl --benchmark frames.jsonl
```

A recording's header names the scene but not the overlay, so its replay takes the same `--overlay` to draw the same meshes (the camera flies the same path either way); the six overlay meshes' first load clusters about ten million triangles into the derived-data cache, and every load after that reads them back.

**Parameters** are tunables, read once as the session starts and written into its header: `view.fly.tick_hz` (240), `view.fly.speed` (metres per second; **0, the default, flies a tenth of the scene's bounding radius a second**, because the same viewer shows a 2 cm mesh and a 5 km desert), `view.fly.fast` (4), `view.fly.slow` (0.25), `view.fly.look` (0.0022 radians per pixel), `view.fly.turn_rate` (2.5 radians per second) and `view.fly.pitch_limit_deg` (89). `--tunable view.fly.speed=20,view.fly.look=0.003` sets them for a run and `--tunables <file.json>` loads a JSON object of them, the file engine-host's `--tunables` takes.

**Why a fixed tick, and not the frame's dt.** A camera integrated over wall-clock frame times is a function of how long each frame happened to take: the same keystrokes flown twice go to two places, a replay can only ever be approximately where the session was, and a benchmark of the replay measures a different path from the one that was flown. At a fixed tick the camera is a function of the tick sequence alone. Every tick is the same `dt`; every event is stamped with the tick it will be fed at; the frames only **read** the state, never write it (a live frame draws it between the last two ticks, below). A frame runs however many ticks a `FixedStepClock` says are due — none when it came sooner than a tick, several after a slow one, and a stall past a quarter of a second is dropped rather than flown through — and the lights and the deformation phase read the tick at a nominal 60 Hz (`FrameDesc::frame_index = tick * 60 / tick_hz`), so they move with simulated time and not with the frame count. The trigonometry is `view::fly_sin_cos`, a polynomial in `f64` that every compiler evaluates to the same bits, because the C library's sine is not the same function on MSVC and glibc; the only libm call turns the start camera into a yaw and a pitch, once, and its answer goes into the header.

**Drawing between ticks.** A live frame draws the camera **between the last two ticks**, at the fraction of a tick the fixed-step clock holds past the last one it ran (`FixedStepClock::interpolation_alpha`): `FlySession::camera_at(alpha)` is `fly_interpolate(previous(), state(), alpha)` — the position on the segment between the two, the pitch between theirs, the yaw the short way round across ±π — so the drawn camera moves by the frame's own time, one tick (4.2 ms) behind the newest state and never ahead of it. Until 2026-09-24 a frame drew the state *at* the last tick boundary, which moves by whole ticks: at 240 Hz against the 82 Hz display of the first owner session that was 3, 3, 3 … 2 — a two-tick frame every 14 — and a near object under a camera turning and strafing at once jumped by a third less on that frame, six times a second, which is the stutter the owner reported and the far scene hides ([the write-up](../experiments/first-interactive-session-2026-09-24.md)). **Only what is drawn moves**: the ticks, the trajectory and its hash, the recording, a replay's frames (four ticks each, tick-aligned by construction), every marker capture and the offscreen `--capture` are exactly what they were, and `tests/fly_tests.cpp` checks every drawn camera of the synthetic session lies on its segment and that asking for them leaves the trajectory's hash alone. What interpolation cannot fix is a frame that samples the clock at the wrong moment: from 3 s into that session the frames came in pairs 1.2 ms apart and 23 ms from the next pair — both presented, a refresh apart, one carrying five or six ticks and the other none or one — and interpolating at the sample time moves the camera by 24 ms per pair however it is drawn. That was present pacing — when a frame samples — and the display pacer below is its fix ([Pacing](#pacing)).

**Why the conversion lives in engine-view.** `foundation/input` must not depend on `foundation/window` (a headless server and a replay have no window), and `foundation/window` stays raw and knows nothing of bindings; the one code that knows both vocabularies is the code that owns both, which is the app — and a game writes this same edge for its own window. Two decisions are made there because that is where the information is: **pointer motion is looking only while the window holds the pointer** (uncaptured, the cursor is pointing, and its motion is dropped at the edge, so it is never fed and never recorded — whether the window held the pointer is already in the log as the events that are there), and **motion and the wheel are summed per tick** (a 1 kHz mouse sends a thousand events a second and the camera reads their sum; summing at the edge gives `InputState` the same total in the same order of additions and keeps a log's size proportional to ticks). Key repeats are dropped for the same reason. **Everything the edge converts is fed at one tick and recorded when it is fed**: a session's recording is exactly what its camera was flown with, and events still waiting for a tick when the window closes are neither.

**`--record-input <log.jsonl>`** writes the session as an `input::InputLog`: the events, the action map's hash, and a **session header** (`engine.view.session`, version `view::k_fly_version`, [The session format](#the-session-format)) in the log header's `session` block ([input](input.md)) — what was drawn (`--scene`, `--mesh`, the procedural scene and its grid, `--grid-instances`), the start camera as a position (three JSON doubles), a yaw and a pitch, the field of view and near plane, every fly parameter above as the session used it, the number of ticks the session ran, and the build stamp of the binary that recorded it. **`--replay-input <log.jsonl>`** flies that log instead of the window: in the window at the recording's own pace (the same fixed-step clock, fed from the file), or offscreen (`--offscreen`, `--benchmark`, `--marker-captures`) at one frame per `tick_hz / 60` ticks, four at 240 Hz, whatever the live frame rate was — a replay benchmark measures the path the player flew at a steady nominal 60 Hz, which is what makes it repeatable, not the frames the player happened to see. It ends when the log's ticks do, or at `--frames`. A replay that names no scene draws the header's; one that names another draws that and says so. `--marker-captures <dir>` writes `tick-NNNNNNN.png` for every tick where `marker` was pressed, each drawn from the marker's own camera and clock after the frames (eight frames at its camera, then the capture), so no grouping of ticks into frames can move one. It is refused together with `--record-input` (a replay is already a recording), `--camera-path` (a replay starts where its recording started), `--animate`, `--morph-animate`, `--reference`, `--fly`, `--census` and `--verify-occlusion` (their own frame loops), and a live session is refused `--offscreen` and `--marker-captures` (nobody can fly a window that is not there, and a live window has no offscreen target to capture from — record, then replay).

### The replay guarantee

**A replay of a recorded session flies the camera the recording flew, bit for bit at every tick, on any machine and any compiler the tree builds for, whatever either run's frame times were — so its marker captures are the recording's to the byte on one machine.** The one thing that can break it is **a change to what the camera computes from the events**: the integration in `fly_camera.cpp` (its arithmetic and its order, the trigonometry, the clamp, the wrap, `frame_index`) or the `InputState` arithmetic it reads, and **the map**. Both are guarded rather than trusted. A log recorded against another action map is refused with both hashes (`input::InputLog::check_map`: the same key would mean another action), and a session header from another integration version is refused rather than misread (`view::k_fly_version`, which a change to the integration bumps) — except version 1, every session recorded before 2026-10-05, which is read and flown as version 2 and says its trajectory is not comparable ([The session format](#the-session-format)). A change that forgot to bump it is caught by the committed trajectory: the synthetic session's hash moves, and `tests/fly_tests.cpp` fails and writes the new trajectory beside its failure. The tunables are not a way to break it — a replay flies with the header's numbers, not today's — and neither is the scene: the camera has no collision, so a replay over another scene flies the same path past different things.

### Far from the origin

**The camera and the walker's feet are `WorldPos`, integrated in f64** ([ADR-0053](../adr/0053-world-positions-are-f64-and-the-gpu-sees-none.md); 2026-10-05). The look, the speeds and **a tick's displacement stay float32** — a tick moves centimetres, which a float holds to a fraction of a micrometre — and the displacement is added to the position in f64: `FlyState::position += DVec3{moved}` in `fly_tick`, and the ground follower's feet the same way in `Walker::step`; the physical walker's feet are the character's own `WorldPos` (`physics::CharacterBody::feet`), no longer narrowed after each step. `fly_interpolate` lerps the position in f64, `Walker::drop`, `step` and `eye` take and give `WorldPos`, and **the camera goes to the renderer as it is**: `renderer::Camera` holds `WorldPos`, `fly_view` sets its position to the state's and its target 100 m along forward added in f64. **Why**: until then both controllers added each 240 Hz tick to an absolute float32. 420 km out a float steps by 3.1 cm, so a walk along x at 1.5 m/s — a 6 mm tick — rounded back to where it was and did not move at all, and the owner's sprint 22° off +z on 2026-10-04 went straight along +z at 3.75 m/s: frames 5256 to 5266 of his frame log show x at −419,055.125 on every frame while z advanced 0.461 m in 0.122 s ([the survey](../experiments/far-from-origin-2026-10-04.md)). At 10,000 km a float steps by a metre and nothing at a walk moved. The seams stage 1 of the change left round them (`camera_point_from_controller` and `camera_point_to_controller` in `view_set.h`, `world_of` and `feet_of` in `walk.cpp`) are gone; what remains is `observer_position` in `walk.cpp` and `anim_lod.h`, the one narrowing to the simulation's float32 observer set, whose 32 m tiles a float's step does not move ([the measurements](../experiments/world-positions-physics-2026-10-05.md#the-controllers-in-f64)).

**What it holds** (`tests/far_tests.cpp`, no GPU, in every configuration — its ground is the test's own provider, a 16 m-periodic swell asked for in whole millimetres, so a site a whole number of 32 m out stands on the origin's ground): the same two seconds of keys and pointer flown from the origin and from 419 km, 10,000 km, 1e8 m and across a 64 m cell's corner at 419 km end **within a micrometre** of the origin's flight moved by the site (measured 44 pm, 81 nm, 0.50 µm and 44 pm), every tick's angles the same bits; the same two and a half seconds walked (a turn, a sprint, a jump, a strafe, back) end within a micrometre at every site (0.2 nm, 0.48 µm, 0.55 µm), and stray at most 7.5 µm mid-walk at the two farther sites, which is the character backend's own double-precision arithmetic (held to 20 µm there, a micrometre at 419 km); **the walker's ground against the ground as drawn** (`walk.max_ground_error_m`: the collision's 1 m heightfield against the world's 25 cm tile lattice) is 0.75 mm at every site, the same as by the origin and not a float's 3.1 cm; **a second's walk along x at 1.5 m/s moves 1.4988 m at 10,000 km**, as it does by the origin (the swell's slope takes the 1.2 mm), where it moved nothing; with a display and a device (`interactive_tests.cpp`), a live session started at `--start 419072.37,10,-419072.61 0,0` and flown forward at 1 m/s for half a second writes frame records whose `pose_position` carries the x typed to the bit (a float holds 419,072.375) and a z that moves by the flight and stands off a float's 3.1 cm grid, where the float32 camera did not move — every record's z on the flight's line at 1 m/s at its own simulated time (`pose_time`, to 0.1 mm) and the last at the session's half second, however many records the window drew (it asked for more than 0.3 m between the first record and the last until 2026-10-07, which a merge gate's load broke: a slow first frame ran sixty ticks, a quarter of a metre down the line, before the first record was drawn, and the 120 ticks came in as few as five records); and **the owner's own sprint**, the walker put at his frame-5256 pose and sprinting at his yaw for the 29 ticks those eleven frames span, moves 0.2317 m along x and 0.5580 m along z — his 5 m/s in the direction he faced, within 0.1 mm — where his frames moved 0 and 0.461. Before the change the flights at 10,000 km and 1e8 m, the walks there and the x walk stood still or moved by whole float steps, and the owner's sprint moved by his frames' numbers.

### The session format

The session header (`engine.view.session`), the trajectory (`engine.view.trajectory`, in the summary's `interactive.trajectory` and in `fly-synthetic.trajectory.json`) and the input log's header that carries the session hold **positions as JSON doubles** — the start camera, the trajectory's `last` and every marker's — and angles as the f64 of their f32, so every number reads back to the same bits. A start outside the world (`world_cell_valid`) is refused. **Version 2** (`view::k_fly_version`, 2026-10-05) is the f64 integration above, its trajectory hash over three doubles a tick; the walk block's version is 2 as well (`view::k_walk_version`: feet in f64, hashed as doubles), and its numbers and the collision's mean what they meant.

**What a session recorded before does** (version 1, float32 positions — every session recorded until 2026-10-05). It **loads and replays**, under version 2's arithmetic: its JSON numbers are the same numbers, its float32 positions exact in a double, its walk block's numbers unchanged. It is not refused, because the owner's recordings are worth replaying and refusing them would buy nothing: the old trajectory cannot be reproduced by this build either way. Its trajectory hash cannot be compared with the recording's, and the replay says so — a line on stderr (`… was recorded with fly camera integration version 1 (float32 positions); this build flies it as version 2 (f64), so its trajectory hash is not the recording's`) and, in the summary's `interactive.trajectory`, `"comparable": false`, `"recorded_version": 1` and a `why` sentence; the summary's `session` block keeps `version: 1`. **By how much it moves**: by the origin, only by the float32 roundings version 1 made — the committed two-second synthetic session (`content/input-logs/sessions/fly-synthetic-v1.jsonl`, the fixture as version 1 wrote it, kept beside the regenerated one) ends **31 µm** from where version 1 ended it (`fly_tests.cpp` holds it under 0.1 mm); far out, by as much as version 1 lost — whole ticks along an axis 420 km out, everything at a walk at 10,000 km — which is the point. A version other than 1 and 2 is refused as before.

**Driving a window with nobody at it.** `--inject-input <log.jsonl>` pushes a log's keys, mouse buttons, pointer motion and wheel onto the window's own event queue (`window::Window::push_event`) as their ticks come due, so they come back out of `poll()` and go through everything a person's input goes through but the operating system: the edge, the tick, the recording. The session ends at the injected log's tick count, or at an Escape pressed with the pointer given back. **Every event lands on its own tick, at any frame rate** (2026-10-07): an event goes onto the queue when its tick is the next to run, and a frame runs no tick past the next event it has not yet pushed, so the frames after a slow one catch the session up with the clock instead. Until then a frame ran every tick that was due, and a slow one — beside a merge gate's builds, a debug window at 256×160 ran sixty ticks a frame — ran past the events whose ticks fell inside it: they went onto the next frame's queue and landed late, and those inside the session's last frame were never fed at all. The live test's fixture presses its last two markers at ticks 450 and 475 of 480, and beside a release build it recorded three or four markers of five in 8 runs of 31 ([the experiment](../experiments/load-sensitive-tests-2026-10-07.md)). Nobody at a window is held this way: their events are polled as they come and fed at the next tick, late by a slow frame but never lost, and a session they end has no tick count to end at. It exists for the end-to-end test and for a smoke run on a shared desktop, where synthesizing keystrokes at the OS level would type into whatever window has focus; an injected session never takes the real pointer, and its capture is only the edge's decision about which motion is looking — it starts holding the pointer and then follows the log's clicks and Escapes, never the desktop's focus. Gamepad and joystick events are not injected (their slots belong to real devices).

**The window's numbers.** Presentation is vsync'd unless `--no-vsync`, and each frame is paced by the display unless `--pace off` ([Pacing](#pacing), below). There is no UI module and no on-screen text path yet, so **the window title** carries them: `engine-view — walking · 12:50 · day 1 h · world ×1 · mouse captured (Esc frees it) — 12.21 ms, p99 13.80 ms (1 s) — tick 4380, recording` — the mode, the sky's hour as the last frame drew it (a scene with a sky; the HUD's clock line), the day's length and the world's rate (`view::format_status`; since 2026-10-20, R88) and the pointer (`mouse free (click to capture, Esc again exits)` once given back) first, rewritten at once when one of them changes, then the last frame's milliseconds (frame start to frame start) and the 99th percentile over the last second (nearest rank over at most 2,048 frames, `view::FramePacing`, fixed storage), the tick and whether it is recording, four times a second; a replay's says `replay tick N of M` and nothing about a pointer. It is set only when its text changed (`view::format_status`, `time_controls.h`). **`--benchmark <out.jsonl>`** on a live session writes the flythrough's JSONL for the frames the window presented — every record with its GPU passes, `cpu_ms` (everything but the waits for a free frame slot and a swapchain image), `frame_ms`, **`ticks`**, how many camera ticks the frame consumed, and the frame's waits and display times ([Pacing](#pacing)) — and ends with the same `FlythroughSummary` line (`path` is `interactive`, or the replayed log), whose `frame_ms.p99` is the session's pacing number, `ticks` its tick total, and `interactive` the session block below. The last two frames' GPU numbers fold in by bringing their slots around with nothing drawn, so every presented frame has a record.

The summary line on stdout gains `"interactive"` — `null` without the flag, otherwise `{mode ("live" or "replay"), map (the action map's hash), record_input, replay_input, inject_input, session (the header, with the ticks the run flew), trajectory}` — where `trajectory` is `{format, version, tick_hz, ticks, hash, last, markers}`: the hash of every tick's camera, bit for bit, where it ended, and each marker's tick and camera — and, for a replay of a version 1 session, `comparable` (false), `recorded_version` and `why` ([The session format](#the-session-format)). Two runs with the same `hash` flew the same path to the last bit; that is how the tests and the smoke below compare them.

**The per-frame path allocates nothing in steady state**: the edge's buffer is sized once (a frame converting more than a thousand events grows it), the `InputState` allocates only the first time it sees each bound code, the tick and the camera are arithmetic, and the title is formatted into a stack buffer (SDL copies it). What grows is what a session asked to keep: `--record-input`'s log and `--benchmark`'s records, both amortized and reserved up front.

**The first smoke** (2026-09-24, `msvc-release`, RTX 5090, under the GPU lock): the desert overlook at 1920×1080 in the window with vsync and `--shadows rt`, started at its camera path's first frame with `--tunable view.fly.speed=20`, the synthetic fixture's keys and pointer motion pushed in with `--inject-input`, recorded and measured. 166 frames in 2.01 s at the display's cadence, **12.21 ms median** (144 frames consumed three ticks each; 480 in all), GPU 0.52 ms median and 0.80 ms p99, CPU 0.38 ms median and 1.94 ms p99. The run's `frame_ms.p99` of **18.2 ms** is two frames: the first one presented after start-up (49.7 ms) and one missed vblank at frame 8 (24.6 ms, with 0.35 ms of CPU and 0.47 ms of GPU in it — the presentation, not the engine); the title's p99 over the last second read 12.8–14.7 ms from the second second on, and it showed Escape's capture toggle arriving at tick 9. The recording (90 events: the fixture's 193, summed per tick by the edge) replayed offscreen twice to the live session's trajectory hash, `3e7c1f84c0959b6a`, and to five marker captures that are the same bytes both times (the last two identical to each other as well: the camera is at the pitch limit, looking at sky). Offscreen at four ticks a frame the same path costs 0.49 ms of GPU median and 0.96 ms p99. **Machine state:** other processes at 18.4–18.7% of the CPU, the GPU 8–12% busy with 6.4–6.5 GB of it in use by other tenants — upper bounds, not costs ([bench](bench.md#measuring-on-a-shared-machine)).

**Without a display** a live session exits 3 with a sentence saying that a recording replays offscreen — which is also what happens on the headless server's `ENGINE_WINDOW_BACKENDS=none` build, where `window::init` fails by design and `--replay-input <log> --offscreen` still runs.

**Testing.** `tools/dev.ps1 test -Filter engine_view` runs `tests/fly_tests.cpp`, `tests/time_controls_tests.cpp` and `tests/interactive_tests.cpp` beside the other end-to-end cases. **Every program engine-view's end-to-end tests start runs on the test process's own desktop** (2026-10-15, after A40; `tests/view_process.h`'s `run_program` through `tests/support/test_desktop.h`, as engine-editor's `run_editor` does): the interactive, injected, replayed and present cases' windows are on nobody's screen and never take the owner's foreground, which they were seen holding during a merge gate, and an offscreen run, which opens no window, is the same run there. Where no desktop can be made (a window station that refuses one) they run on the default desktop as before and the first run says why; off Windows the helper is `platform::Process` alone. **With no GPU and no window**: the default map equals the committed file byte for byte, is seventeen actions to its third revision's fourteen, its second's twelve and its first's eight, and names every action the camera reads; the committed synthetic session names the first revision and is found by that hash; the two ladders are the ones above, a key steps a rung from a rung and from between two and stops at the ends, the sun's day moves by the rate and never jumps when it changes, a rate is written `86,400` and `5,000.5`, and the title's status is the UTF-8 text above, cut rather than overrun in a small buffer; the fly session counts `[` `]` `,` `.` once a press, a held key once, with the second revision and not at all with the first, the camera untouched either way, and a control of the wrong kind is refused; `fly_sin_cos` is within 2.5e-7 of the C library over two turns, exactly odd and even, and the camera at rest for an angle that is not finite; forward and right are `rotate(quat_from_euler(yaw, pitch, 0), -z / +x)` at five orientations, a start taken from a camera looks where it looked, and every action moves the camera the way the table says (W two metres in a second at 2 m/s, Shift four times that, Alt a quarter, W with D no faster than W, E up whatever the pitch, a pointer pull past the pole stopping at the limit, yaw wrapping within a turn, a stick turning at its rate); the committed synthetic session is exactly what `make_synthetic_session` writes and flies **the committed trajectory** (`content/input-logs/sessions/fly-synthetic.trajectory.json`) whether it is cut into ticks of one, four, seven, 33 or 480; the same session as version 1 wrote it is read, flown as version 2, ends 31 µm from version 1's end and does not claim its hash; every one of its 2,400 drawn cameras (each tick at five fractions) lies on the segment between the last two ticks — within a micrometre of the line and 2e-4 of its fraction along it — with the yaw taking the short way across ±π, and a session asked for them after every tick flies the same hash; a rebound map and another integration version are refused with their messages; the edge's conversion, its per-tick sums and repeat drop, and `--inject-input`'s reverse direction; and the title's percentile. **Why the trajectory is the GPU-free half**: the guarantee is about the camera, the pictures follow from it, and comparing pictures needs a device most CI machines lack — the hash is the camera itself, needs a CPU and nothing else, and is committed from Windows, so GCC, Clang and both CPU baselines each prove on every build that they fly the recording to the same place. **End to end**, on every machine: the flag combinations that cannot work together exit 2 (among them a `--sun-rate` out of range or not a number, `--start` malformed, on a replay or beside `--camera-path`, `--walk` without a live session, and `--capture-channels` on a window, with an unknown name or with nothing to capture), and a replay of a missing file, of a device log from engine-input (no session), under a rebound map, and from a future integration version each exit 1 with its message before a device is asked for. **With a device**: the version 1 fixture replays offscreen and its summary's trajectory says `"comparable": false`; the fixture replayed twice offscreen with `--marker-captures` writes five PNGs that are the same bytes both times, both summaries carry the committed trajectory, and the `--benchmark` JSONL has 120 records of four ticks each and a summary counting 480; **with a display and a device**: a live window fed the fixture through `--inject-input` records a log (the default map's hash, 480 ticks, `recorded_by` engine-view) whose offscreen replay flies **the live session's trajectory to the last bit** — the live path and the replay path agree, which is the guarantee — and its live `--benchmark` has a record for every presented frame, their ticks adding up to 480; and a live session over a scene file that names a camera path starts at its first frame (read back from the recording's header: the key's x and z, over the ground, looking at its target), and one given `--start 1,-2,3.5 90,-10` starts exactly there, 270° coming back as -90°; **the keys**: an injected session over a small dune terrain at `--time-rate 0`, pressing `.` once and `]` twice, Escape, a click, Escape and Escape, eighty ticks apart, ends at the last Escape rather than at its log's 800 ticks with its recording written, and its summary says the dunes went from 0 to 60 in one change and the sun from 0 to 600 in two, as its two console lines do; and **both rates offscreen**, `--time-rate 86400 --sun-rate 3600 --frames 12 --capture` on the same terrain, carries a `time_lapse` block at 86,400 and a `sun` block at 3,600, 660 game seconds into the day and climbing, while a still day ends where it started and no `--time-rate` has no time-lapse. Each case skips where the app exits 3; the two over the dune terrain skip too in a build whose engine-view has no terrain capability (`msvc-minimal`), which refuses that scene with the scene-generator registry's sentence — and only there, since the test target is told at build time whether the capability is linked (`ENGINE_VIEW_TESTS_DUNES`), so a refusal in a full build still fails them. They failed that way on a minimal build with a device until 2026-09-27, when this was added; a minimal run with no device, as CI's, skips at the exit 3 first. **The live sessions in these tests still ask for `--no-vsync`, under the ceiling** ([Pacing](#pacing)). Dropping the flag was tried first (2026-09-28) and is the wrong fix: a window paced by its display runs at the display's rate, which falls to a few frames a second when the display sleeps or the desktop is locked, and at that rate two cases lost markers (three of five recorded, and the walk's `stopped` marker missing) — so a vsync'd test passes at a desk and fails on a machine nobody is sitting at. An unthrottled window under the ceiling depends on neither. (The markers were lost by the injector, not by the rate: a slow frame ran past events it had not pushed, which a loaded machine did to the unthrottled window too until the injector stopped doing it on 2026-10-07 — [`--inject-input`](#--interactive-a-camera-somebody-flies), above. The flag stays; a vsync'd run has not been tried since.)

### Walking

**The owner had only ever flown over the desert** (2026-09-28): to judge a dune's scale from eye height, walk up a slip face and be stopped by a ruin's wall, the camera has to stand on something. **F** switches the session between flying and walking (`walk.h`, `walk.cpp`); **`--walk`** starts a live session walking. F was bound to nothing — W A S D, E and Q, Space and the modifiers, Escape, M and the four rate keys were — and is where a hand on W A S D already is.

- **Walking** puts the camera at eye height on a walker, the look exactly as in flight (the pointer and the right stick, `fly_look`, the first half of `fly_tick` with its arithmetic untouched), **W A S D along the ground in the look's heading** — the camera's forward with its pitch taken out, from `fly_sin_cos` — **Shift to sprint** (the `fast` action), **Space to jump** (and a pad's South). Space is also `lift`'s: a flying camera reads `lift` and never `jump`, a walking one the reverse, so one key means the obvious thing in each. On a pad the left stick walks and its click sprints, which fell out of the actions already bound; the switch itself is the keyboard's.
- **Switching to walking drops the walker onto the ground under the camera**: the highest surface below it — a ruin's wall top, if the camera is over one — or, from under the ground, the surface above; **switching to flying leaves the camera where it is**, and it flies on from the eye. A console line says each switch (`engine-view: walking (physics)`, `engine-view: flying`), and **the window title says which mode it is in**, first: `walking`, `walking (ground only)` or `flying` (`view::TitleMode`, `format_status`).
- **What a `--walk` start guarantees**: the session stands on the ground under its start camera before its first tick — the drop above, from wherever `--start` put the camera, 60 m up included — and W A S D walk it from that first tick, with no F pressed. On 2026-09-29 the owner reported a `--walk` session that ignored W A S D until F flew and F walked again; it did not reproduce — his five recordings of the day each moved the walker at walking speed from the first tick W was held, and his start replayed with W held from tick 1 (the committed erg whole, `msvc-release`, three views, the maps) walked 2.99 m in two seconds — and the two things his runs did that a session can look like that after are written up in [the experiment](../experiments/walk-in-time-lapse-2026-09-29.md#3-w-a-s-d-at-a---walk-start): a walker the rising sand had closed over (below; fixed) walks where nobody can see it until F flies it out, and the window's first captured pointer motion can carry the cursor's whole distance to the window's centre (in one of his sessions it turned the camera 346° and pitched it 68° up, into the sky, before the first frame). The end-to-end case below holds the start.
- **On moving sand the walker goes with the ground** ([scene_collision](scene_collision.md#the-walker-goes-with-the-ground)). The collision under the walker follows the drawn sand to a millimetre, a frame at a time, and before each step the walker is carried by what that changed under its feet when it stands on the ground, and lifted onto the collision ground when the sand has risen past its feet — so its eye, which is the camera, rises and sinks with the sand and never steps, and rising sand never closes over it. It is left standing with no velocity: a fall or a jump the sand caught ends as a landing does, and the input sets the horizontal speed every step, so walking up a rising dune keeps walking. Until 2026-09-29 a standing walker's eye stood still over sinking sand and dropped 4.65 cm in a frame each time the collision caught up (the owner's jump about once a second at 600 game seconds a real second), and sand rising round a standing walker buried its feet, and from a few thousand game seconds a real second went over the capsule and dropped it through the dune (his fall at a week a second).
- **Two walkers, one interface** (`view::Walker`). Where the build carries the physics and scene-collision capabilities, the walker is a **`physics::CharacterBody`** ([physics](physics.md#the-character)) — a 1.8 m capsule of 0.3 m radius with its eye at 1.65 m, a 40° slope limit past which it slides and which it cannot walk up, a 0.35 m step it climbs, gravity and a jump — on the static bodies **`scene_collision::SceneCollision`** keeps round its feet: the ground as a heightfield a tile at 1 m from the scene's ground provider, the ruins' walls and rubble as a compound a tile of each piece's coarse level ([scene_collision](scene_collision.md)). **With physics off** (`ENGINE_WITH_PHYSICS=OFF`, or `msvc-minimal`, where every capability is) the walker **follows the ground**: the feet on the provider's height — asked at the nearest whole millimetre of the feet as an i64 (`scene_gen::GroundProvider::height_mm`), never through a float32 metre or a 1 mm lattice's i32 index, which overflowed past 2,147 km ([scene_gen](scene_gen.md#far-from-the-origin), 2026-10-05) — a jump an arc of the header's gravity back onto it, and nothing that stands on the ground in the way — the title says `walking (ground only)` and the summary's `walk.collision` is `ground-follow`, with `why` saying so. A scene with no terrain (a `--mesh`, the procedural heightfield) has nothing to walk on: F does nothing there, and `--walk` says so on the console and flies.
- **The collision follows the walker's ticks, not the frames.** The collision ring is the walker's own — one ring of 1.5 tiles round its feet — updated once a **tick**, before the step, from the walker's own position, never once a frame from the camera: a character's contacts are sorted by body id and body ids are handed out in the order bodies are made, so bodies made at frame boundaries — which a live window and its offscreen replay place at different ticks — would make a replay walk somewhere else ([scene_collision](scene_collision.md#why-this-shape)). A streamed scene (`--world`) keeps its drawing ring for the camera beside it; the walker's is separate.
- **A recorded walk replays bit for bit.** The map's third revision (below) is recorded with the session, the session header gains a **`walk` block** — the walk integration's version (`view::k_walk_version`), the walker's numbers and the collision's, and whether it started walking — so a replay walks with the recording's numbers whatever the tunables say now, and a header from another walk version is refused rather than walked somewhere else — except version 1, which is walked as version 2 with its session ([The session format](#the-session-format)). The trajectory hash marks every walking tick, and the summary carries the walker's own state chain (`walk.hash`: the character's, or the ground follower's); a replay over the same scene reproduces both, which the end-to-end cases below hold. The camera's own guarantee still holds for flight over any scene; a walk is a function of the scene too, since the ground and the walls decide it. **On moving sand** the collision follows the ground the renderer draws, which a live window's display clock drives, so a walk under a live time-lapse replays against the sand the replay draws; at the game's own rate the sand does not move and the walk replays exactly.
- **Wading** (2026-10-17, roadmap S63; [water](water.md#wading)). Where the scene has water, the walker asks the water capability for the depth at its feet before every step (`Walker::set_water`, attached in `main.cpp` where the build has the capability, `ENGINE_VIEW_WATER`) and scales its speed by a declared curve — full to the ankle (0.3 m), half by the waist (1 m), a quarter at the chest (1.4 m) — and at the chest a step that leads deeper is not taken: the walker stops and wades back out. The water's own mesh is no collision surface, so the walker stands on the ground under it. A scene without water attaches nothing and walks the floats it always did; the summary's `walk` block gains `wading` (`max_depth_m`, `ticks`, `stopped_ticks`) only where there is water.
- **The map's third revision** (`view::k_fly_map_revision` 3, 2026-09-28) appends `walk` (F) and `jump` (Space, a pad's South) after the time-lapse's four keys. A log recorded against the first or second revision replays with that revision (`view::default_fly_map_for`), its F pressing nothing, so every session recorded before today flies exactly as it did ([input](input.md)); its header has no `walk` block and reads as a session that starts flying.

| Tunable | Default | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| `view.walk.height` | 1.8 | the walker's capsule, feet to crown, metres |
| `view.walk.radius` | 0.3 | its radius |
| `view.walk.eye_height` | 1.65 | the camera over the feet |
| `view.walk.speed` | 1.5 | walking, metres a second |
| `view.walk.sprint` | 5.0 | with Shift held |
| `view.walk.max_slope_deg` | 40 | steeper ground is slid down and cannot be walked up (the erg's slip faces stand at 34°, so they can be climbed) |
| `view.walk.step_height` | 0.35 | a ledge this high is climbed by walking into it (a fallen block, not a wall) |
| `view.walk.gravity` | 9.81 | metres a second squared |
| `view.walk.jump_speed` | 4.0 | upward at the start of a jump: 0.82 m high |
| `walk.collision.*` | | the collision's spacing, ring radius, budget, proxy and ground errors and refresh budget ([scene_collision](scene_collision.md#the-tunables)) |

**The summary's `walk` block** (in `interactive`, for a live session and a replay alike): `collision` (`physics`, `ground-follow`, or `none` with no terrain) and `why`, `version` and `params` (the header's numbers), `start` and `mode` (`fly` or `walk`: how the session started and where it ended), `mode_changes` (F presses that switched it), `available`, `ticks` walked, `distance_m` walked on the ground (horizontally) and `climb_m` climbed (by the walker's own steps: moving sand carrying it up is not climbing), `drops`, **`max_ground_error_m`** — the largest distance at the walker's feet between the ground it stood on (the collision heightfield, or the provider's height when it follows the ground) and **the ground as drawn** (the scene's grid under it as the renderer triangulates a quad, at the drawn time) — and `hash`; with physics, `bodies` — `live` now, `max`, `created`, `destroyed`, `tiles` held, `activated` and `deactivated`, `placements` and `max_placements` (pieces in the tiles' compounds), `proxies` and `proxy_triangles`, whether the placements came from `streamed` generators, and the backend's `ground_bytes`, `compound_bytes` and `proxy_bytes` — `ground` (`samples` held on the CPU, `refreshes`, `field_evaluations`, `max_stale_m`, `max_kept_m`: the time-lapse's rule, [scene_collision](scene_collision.md#the-ground-moves); and `carries`, `max_carry_m`, `lifts`, `max_lift_m`: the ticks the moving sand carried the walker and the most in one, and the times it had risen past the walker's feet by more than a tenth of a millimetre and the most, [scene_collision](scene_collision.md#the-walker-goes-with-the-ground)) and `ms` (`activate_total`, `activate_max`, `deactivate_total`, `refresh_total`, `refresh_max`, `proxy_total`).

**Testing.** With no GPU and no window (`tests/walk_tests.cpp`, over a small terrain of the renderer's own waves, so in every configuration — a character where the build has physics, the ground follower in `msvc-minimal`): F walks onto the ground under the camera and F again flies from where it stands; a second at a walk goes 1.5 m north, half a second's sprint 2.5 m more, the feet on the ground to 2 cm (the backend's padding; exactly with the follower) and the walker's ground error under 4e-5 m against the drawn grid; the same events twice are the same trajectory and the same walker; `--walk` starts on the ground and a jump rises by v²/2g to within 1% and lands after 2v/g; out and back over the same ground ends where it started; a session whose map has no `walk` flies to exactly the trajectory it had without the F presses, and so does one with no walker or none over a terrain; the header's `walk` block reads back and another walk version is refused; the title leads with the mode; and, where the build has the dunes and physics, the walker the window runs, standing on the committed erg at the owner's start with the dunes at 600 and handed the ground as the renderer's own model draws it a frame at a time, moves its eye at most 1.03 mm a frame against the sand's 0.14 and within 0.9 mm of the sand's own move every frame (before 2026-09-29 it stood still and dropped 4.65 cm in a frame, three times in the case's 1,200 frames). **With a display and a device** (`tests/walk_view_tests.cpp`): an injected session on **the committed erg** — the erg's own dunes, its bands, seed and time, drawn over a 512 m window of it at 2 m, because a Debug engine-view takes more than ten minutes to build the 6 km grid's DAG and the dunes are a function of position and not of the grid — starts walking on the interdune floor at x = 140, five metres short of where the mega-draa's slip face rises at the committed time (the camera path's `toe` marker, x = 100, is 45 m out on the floor looking at it), sprints four seconds east up the face and four back down: on 2026-09-28 it went 16.7 m east and **6.5 m up the face** (the eye from 16.1 m to 22.6), came back down onto the floor 3.3 m past its start and within 0.52 m of its height, 36.7 m walked in all, the ground under its feet **at most 9.5 cm off the drawn one** — the drawn grid here is the window's 2 m and the collision's a metre, so on the curved face the two triangulations of the same sand differ by about that; over the committed erg's 1.5 m grid it is less; its recording replays offscreen to the live trajectory and the live walker's hash, bit for bit; and a session started with the owner's own flags over the same window of the erg — `--walk` from 60 m over x = 140, z = −60 looking north, `--views surround3`, `--shadows csm` — holding W from its first tick has walked 1.50 m north after a second of ticks (2026-09-29). And (`tests/walk_ruins_view_tests.cpp`, where the ruins are) on **the committed `content/test-scenes/ruins-walk`** — one building of the kit of boxes, which the test writes beside a copy of the scene — a walker started five metres outside the building's longest full-height wall, facing it, holds W for six seconds (nine metres at a walk) and **stops 0.32 m in front of the wall's face** — the capsule's 0.3 m radius and the backend's 2 cm padding (2026-09-28, a 2 m section of full height); where physics is off the follower walks on through, and the case asserts that instead; it replays offscreen to the same trajectory and hash. And (`tests/walk_limb_view_tests.cpp`, where the flora capability is) on **the committed `content/test-scenes/elder-grove`**, copied with its recipes into the test's scratch directory: the test solves each ancient tree's recipe to find its lowest major limb's straight run from the structural table, and a session it writes starts a walker on the run 2.5 m out from the bark, facing along it, W held, **replayed offscreen** (no window): on the near-level limb, 30 m up, it walks the whole 19.4 m run with its feet within half a millimetre of the bark and 1.4 mm aside, which the case holds for the first 15 m; on the second tree's limb, rising at 20°, it climbs, and the case holds its first 8 m (flora.md, "Walking a limb", has what it does past them). Every case skips where the app exits 3, the erg's also where the build has no dunes.

**Not yet**, and deliberately out of this change: the character rig (bone capsules, foot placement, footprints in the sand), swimming (wading is above; S86), crouching, NPC locomotion, anything over the protocol (`render.*` does not walk), gamepad bindings beyond what fell out of the actions already bound (the switch is keys-only), walking on a scene with no terrain (the walker needs a ground provider; a mesh's own collision would be the next step), a collision ground that uses the rings' finer drawn lattice or the world's ground tiles with their footprint overlays, and bit-for-bit replay of a walk over sand a live time-lapse moves.

### Pacing

**What sets a window's frame time** (2026-09-25, [the second owner session](../experiments/second-interactive-session-2026-09-25.md#present-pacing)). With FIFO the display is the slowest part of the loop, and the frame waits for it in one of three places: for its frame slot (`begin_frame`: the GPU finishing the frame two back, which on this machine's driver cannot start until the display releases its swapchain image), for an image (`acquire`, which on NVIDIA's Windows driver returns at once and leaves the GPU to wait), or in the present call (which blocks while the driver's queue of presents is full). Two throttles make two stable rhythms: **even**, every present blocking about 9–11 ms, and **paired**, one present returning at once and the next iteration waiting 14 ms for its slot and 9 ms in the present — frames starting 0.6 ms and 23.7 ms apart, a pair every two refreshes. The cameras the two draw, read after the slot wait, stand 15 ms and 9 ms apart instead of 12.2, now and then one barely after the other; and the window's input is polled only every 24 ms, so a turn reaches every other frame, out of step with a strafe that reaches every frame — which is the owner's "one frame in between is in a previous position" while strafing with the mouse on an asset ([the strafing stutter](../experiments/second-interactive-session-2026-09-25.md#the-strafing-stutter)). A hitch moves the loop from the first to the second and nothing moves it back: the first owner session was even for 3 s and paired for the remaining 3.5 minutes; the second was paired from its 22nd frame. Behind either rhythm the queue is deep: 55–60 ms, four and a half to five refreshes, from a frame sampling its input to its reaching the display. **The owner's configuration until now — FIFO, three images, two frames in flight, no pacer — is the pairing one**: replayed in the spanned window, 243 of 1,610 frames came more than 20 ms after the one before.

**The display pacer** (`--pace`, default `auto`). Before a frame polls its window and reads the clock, the loop waits until the frame it presented last has been shown (`gfx::Swapchain::wait_for_present`, VK_KHR_present_wait2). Each frame then starts one refresh after the one before, on the display's own clock, with one frame queued, and the three waits above find nothing to wait for: the slot, the image and the present each take under 0.1 ms. Replayed in the spanned window on the final build, 0 and 4 of 1,610 frames came more than 20 ms apart, the frame time was 12.2 ms median and 13.7–14.4 ms p95, every frame reached the display one refresh (12.21 ms) after the one before whenever the compositor flipped the window straight to the display, and each frame reached it within about one refresh of sampling its input instead of nearly five — 46.5 ms sooner, by the display times. The window is polled right after the wait, so input reaches every frame too: in a live session strafing at 30 m/s while turning, each displayed frame strafed 0.32–0.41 m and turned within 0.17° (p95) of what its own time step called for, where the unpaced loop alternated 0.28 m with 0.46 m and turned out of step by up to 0.56°. **How far back it waits is `view::DisplayPacer`'s** (`pacing.h`): depth 1 while the frames make their refresh; two misses within 32 waits — a wait that came a refresh and a half after the last, not made up by an early one after it — mean the work no longer fits, and it waits for the present before the last instead (depth 2: a refresh more latency, a refresh more room), tries depth 1 again after 240 frames, and doubles that hold each time the try fails at once. A frame with 8.3 ms of CPU and GPU work between sampling and presenting still made every refresh at depth 1 at 82 Hz, so depth 2 is for work past about a refresh. `auto` paces the FIFO-family modes (FIFO, FIFO-relaxed, FIFO-latest-ready) and leaves mailbox and immediate unthrottled, since that is what asking for them means; `display` paces any mode; `off` is the loop as it was. A surface that cannot wait on a present is paced by the swapchain's own waits, with a warning. Presents nobody can see (a minimized window's) cost two of the wait's 50 ms timeouts, after which the pacer stops waiting for 120 frames and then looks again.

**In a composed window it trades cadence for latency.** A window that does not cover the display is composed by DWM, which returns a present wait anywhere from about 5 to 20 ms after the last, and whose own display cadence is uneven either way (one frame in six reaches the display a few milliseconds late and the next early, paced or not). There the pacer spends most of its time at depth 2 and cuts the sample-to-display time by about 44 ms (68 ms to 24 ms as the display times report them, which read early), at the cost of the frames sampling on an uneven clock: 12.6 ms median but 18.6 ms p95, where the unpaced loop samples every 12.2 ms. `--pace off` is the even-cadence choice there.
